husband encouraged her to see their primary care physician, whose concerns about her weight loss led to a psychiatric referral.\n- Chief Complaint: Lately I feel like how my body looks and what I weigh are all I can focus on when I think about whether I’m doing well in life.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S043 (Restrictive_Intake): Most days I tell my family I already ate at my middle I’ll finally look how I’m supposed to as a supervisor and performer.\n- S045 (Fear_of_Weight_Gain): If the scale goes up even half a pound after a weekend rehearsal or if I eat something (Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance): When I’m judging how I’m doing in life, it’s not about being a good mom or handling the promotion anymore—it's all about whether my stomach looks flat in my work It has just started to take over how I judge myself every day.\n\nDoctor: To help me understand better, have you been strictly limiting the amount of food you eat, or do you sometimes experience episodes where you feel you lose control and eat a very large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I definitely strictly limit what I eat; I don't have times where I lose control and eat large amounts. I immediately feel this urgent need to make up for it by cutting back even more on my food the next day.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor or healthcare professional recently told you that your weight is dangerously low or that you are medically underweight?\n\nPatient: Yes, my primary care doctor was very concerned about my weight loss and said my weight was too low, which is actually why she referred me here. Honestly, though, I think she and my husband are exaggerating the danger because I still have plenty of energy to work my shifts and go jogging. To me, it just feels like I'm finally being disciplined about my body.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned not paying much attention to your cycle, but looking back, have your menstrual periods stopped completely for the past three months or more?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, to be honest. With all the stress of my promotion and trying to help my parents back home, I haven't kept track of my periods at all. I just haven't paid close attention to it lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you happen to remember what weight your doctor recorded at your recent visit, or could you share your height and what your typical weight used to be?\n\nPatient: I don't remember the exact weight my doctor wrote down, and I'm not really sure of my exact height or what I used to weigh. I just know that lately all of my clothes have been hanging off me.\n\nDoctor: Even though you haven't been keeping close track, do you remember having any menstrual bleeding at all in the last three months?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't recall. With everything going on with my promotion and my parents, I really haven't been paying attention to it at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you currently taking any birth control pills or using other hormonal contraceptives?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I am not currently taking any birth control pills or using other hormonal contraceptives. I haven't been on anything like that for quite some time now.

I haven't been on anything like that for quite some time now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other physical changes, such as your hair thinning, or the growth of fine, downy hair on your arms or face?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any hair thinning or any fine hair growing on my arms or face. I'm really not sure about those specific physical changes, as I haven't seen anything like that happening."

I just know that lately all of my clothes have been hanging off me.\n\nDoctor: Even though you haven't been keeping close track, do you remember having any menstrual bleeding at all in the last three months?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't recall. With everything going on with my promotion and my parents, I really haven't been paying attention to it at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you currently taking any birth control pills or using other hormonal contraceptives?\n\nPatient: No, I am not currently taking any birth control pills or using other hormonal contraceptives. I haven't been on anything like that for quite some time now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other physical changes, such as your hair thinning, or the growth of fine, downy hair on your arms or face?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any hair thinning or any fine hair growing on my arms or face. I'm really not sure about those specific physical changes, as I haven't seen anything like that happening.\n\nDoctor: Did your primary care doctor happen to mention what your BMI, or Body Mass Index, was during your recent visit?\n\nPatient: I don't recall my doctor mentioning a specific BMI number during my visit. She only told me that she was very concerned about my weight loss and that I was too thin."

Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future. Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
